2026. 10.27
先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して
いると報告ベージュブック
（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め
を試みたが、供給業者は他案件への供給を優先
し、これを拒否
機械製造業は、DC向けに鋼材在庫が大量に消

の建設案件の遅延を招いていると指摘
テキサス州
見通し調査
（2026/9）
製造業では原材料コストが上昇。特に鋼材価格
が急上昇しており、その一因はDC需要とされるベージュブック
（2026/9） 一部の製造業がアルミニウム・銅・鋼材の供給不
足や調達期間の長期化を報告
一部の自動車メーカーはアルミニウムや一部部品
の供給不足で生産が幾分抑制されたと報告

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を嫌気。不透明感は2027年入り後も継続する見込み
8
 金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を警戒。主要国の選挙を控える中、不透明感は2027年まで継続へ
― 抗議活動が広がるイタリア、フランス、ベルギーの一般政府債務残高（GDP比）は、EU加盟国の中でも上位（それぞれ2
位、3位、4位）。金利上昇の背景は政治・財政への懸念
― 影響は為替市場にも波及。9月中旬以降、ユーロはドルに対して弱含み
―2027年にかけてフランス大統領選やスペイン・イタリアの総選挙など、欧州主要国で重要な国政選挙が相次ぐ。極右・ポ

Document 085: 2026年7-9月期GDP予測(最終版) ～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期GDP予測(最終版) ～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 11 月10 日

1/2 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測(最終版) 
～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

11 月16 日に公表される26 年７-９月期の実質
ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率▲0.4％（前期比▲
0.1％）と予測する。10 月 30 日の段階では前期
比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋0.1％）と小幅プラス
成長を予想していたが、その後公表された経済
指標の結果を反映し、小幅マイナス成長へと予
測値を下方修正する。 
本日公表された26 年９月分の国際収支統計の
結果を反映し、26 年７-９月期の実質輸出を前期
比＋0.1％（従来予測値：同＋1.2％）へ下方修
正する。筆者の９月分の想定と比べてサービス
輸出が大きく下振れたことが主因である。実質
輸入も前期比＋3.0％（従来予測値：同＋
3.3％）と下方修正するが、輸出の下方修正度合
いの方が大きいことから、外需の予測値を前期
比年率寄与度▲1.9％Pt（従来予測値：同▲
1.3％Pt）へ下方修正している。 
また、９月分の家計調査と家計消費状況調査
が想定よりも下振れたことを反映し、個人消費
の予測値も前期比▲0.3％と、従来予測値の同▲
0.2％から小幅下方修正した。

長が続いた後であることや、企業部門の底堅さが維持されていることを踏まえれば、景気回復の
流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろう。
物価高による節約志向は根強く、個人消費の回復にはなお時間を要するとみられるが、設備投
資を中心に企業部門が好調さを維持している点は好材料である。家計部門が力強さを欠く一方、
企業部門が景気を下支えする構図のもと、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると判
断している。

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/3 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅ながら４四半期連続のプラス成長を予想、景気回復基調は維持 
11 月16 日に公表される2026 年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋
0.1％）と予測する。４四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
一方、設備投資が高い伸びになることが下支えになる見込みである。設備投資の伸びには特殊要
因も一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。個人消費が弱含むなかでも
プラス成長を維持するとみられることは、企業部門の底堅さが景気を下支えしていることを示し
ている。過去３四半期に比較的高い成長が続いた後であることも踏まえれば、今回の減速をもっ
て景気の基調が弱まったと判断する必要はないだろう。家計部門の弱さは懸念材料だが、企業部
門の底堅さは維持されており、景気は引き続き緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
７-９月期のＧＤＰを評価する際には、原油輸入と公的在庫の動きをセットで捉える必要があ
る。４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を
通じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油
が放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt
（前期比年率寄与度▲2.1％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げ
は、公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じるとみられる。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって
原油輸入が回復するため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因になる。一方、輸入の回復に
伴って国家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小す
るとみられる。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.4％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋1.5％
Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し上げることが予想される。

1 10 月 30 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果
を踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げと公的在庫変動による押し上げは、ともに原油の供給環境が改
善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一
体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクが低下していることである。
ＧＤＰ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用
可能な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むし
ろ前向きに捉えるべきだろう。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減少を予想する。８～９月に各地で相次いだ大雨や台風などの
天候不順が外出機会やサービス消費を抑制した可能性がある。また、４-６月期には自動車やエア
コンなどの耐久財販売が増加したが、７-９月期にはこうした一時的な押し上げ効果が剥落した。
加えて、物価高による節約志向が根強いなか、食料品や日用品などの非耐久財消費にも弱さがみ
られる。高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境は下支え要因となっているものの、天候不順という一時的
な要因もあって個人消費は総じて力強さを欠く結果になったとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と、高い伸びを予想する。４-６月期には特許権の海外譲渡に伴う研
究開発投資の減少という特殊要因があったが、７-９月期はその影響が剥落することが押し上げ要
因になった面はある。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を除いても設備投資は明確に増加したと考え
られ、底堅い動きとなっている。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応した省力化
投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資への需要は根強く、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強い。設備投
資計画も高い伸びとなっており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断してよ
いだろう。 
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れとなる見
込みである。両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。 
輸出は前期比＋1.2％と４四半期連続の増加を予想する。４-６月期に大口要因によって研究開
発サービスの受取が急増した分が剥落することで、サービス輸出が減少する一方、財輸出が高い
伸びとなり、財貨・サービス輸出全体では増加を維持したとみられる。半導体製造装置などの資
本財輸出が好調だった。地域別では、ＥＵ向けが低調だった一方、ＡＳＥＡＮ、ＮＩＥｓ向けの
資本財輸出が伸びている。また、４-６月期に急減した中東向け輸出が持ち直したことも押し上げ
につながっている。 
輸入は前期比＋3.3％と大幅な増加が予想される。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が
大幅に減少していたが、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄
与度は前期比▲0.3％Pt（前期比年率▲1.3％Pt）になったと予想される。 
ただし、この外需寄与度のマイナスは、資源輸入の回復に伴う輸入の増加によって生じるもの

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
である。その裏側では公的在庫変動が前期比＋0.4％ポイント、前期比年率では＋1.5％ポイント
の大幅プラス寄与になると見込まれる。両者は併せて評価する必要がある。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.1％Ptと予想する。製品在庫はプラスが予想される一方、流通
在庫の下押しが大きくなるとみられる。 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.9％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因
となっており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。また、公共投資も同▲1.5％と減少を
予想するほか、政府消費についても、４-６月期の高い伸びの反動から前期比▲0.3％と減少を見
込む。

GUC (3443.TW)

GUC
Company description
Founded in 1998, Global Unichip (GUC) specializes in providing IC design
services, particularly in ASIC service. As a subsidiary of TSMC, which holds
34.8% of GUC, it offers a wide range of design services including spec-in and
SoC integration, physical implementation, 2.5D/3D packaging technologies,
mass production services, and advanced technologies such as high
bandwidth memory (HBM) and chip to chip interconnect IP .  With its

diversified global presence – Taiwan, North America, China, Europe, South
Korea, and Japan – the company offers services including mass production,
NRE, customer wafer verification projects, and IP licensing. Mass production
services provide customers with complete service, from back-end design
completion to wafer manufacturing, testing and management.
Investment strategy
We rate GUC stock as Buy/High Risk. We are seeing strong growth from its
CPU projects with two leading CSP customers. We also see strong order

potential from its EV clients. Long-term, we still believe back-end design
capability and 3DIC system integration will be critical to AI accelerators,
which would give better opportunities for TSMC 3DFabric partners with GUC
also being a beneficiary, in our view.
Valuation
Our target price for GUC of NT$8,000 (rounded) is based on 37x PER to the
average of our 2027 /28E EPS, slightly above its historical 3-year PER average
which we believe is justified by its strong earnings outlook supported by solid

demand from its AI projects with two US leading CSPs and growth upside
from its US EV clients. Our target price equates to 2027 /28E PB of 30x/14x.
Risks
Our quant risk rating system rates the GUC stock High Risk for its share-price
volatility. Downside risks that could mean the stock trades below our target
price include: 1) slower projects demand from US CSP customers, 2) lower-
than-expected NRE demand from less AI ASIC projects, and 3) delayed or less

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 1

M
Idea
Wiwynn Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q beat on higher GM/OpM
Recent concerns revolve around potential data center delays,
though the supply chain has not seen any changes. However, we
lower our mid-term growth  rate to bake in some conservatism.
Our PT now implies a lower CY27 P/E at  12x. RR looks attractive
at 7.7x CY27 P/E vs. the 3Y  avg. of ~10x.
4Q revenue should continue to grow double-digits q/q: Wiwynn announced
October revenue at ~NT$165B (-4% m/m, +81% y/y). If we assume the October

monthly run-rate can be sustained, 4Q should grow at least 13-14% q/q. However,
we think ASIC server racks will continue to ramp-up through-out year-end, so we
forecast 4Q revenue to ~20% q/q to reach ~NT$520B (+78% y/y). Higher cost
structure and higher mix of ASIC server racks should drive margins to decline q/q to
~8.5% (-40bps q/q), resulting in net profit of ~NT$27.7B or EPS of NT$46.70.
ASIC/GPU projects remain on track: Our checks indicate that  Trainium3 server rack

production is ramping up well at Wiwynn, and with T3, Wiwynn is a more
comprehensive partner, also engaging in UBB and switch trays. It will also engage in
AMD's (covered by Joe Moore) MI455 Helios rack assembly for customers such as
Meta (covered by Brian Nowak) and Oracle (covered by Sanjit Singh). We forecast
Wiwynn to build ~8K Helios racks for CY27.
3Q results beat on better margins: Revenue was NT$434.6B (+63% y/y) with GM
of 8.9% (-40bps q/q), which is 90bps/30bps above MSe/Street. OP reached ~NT

$32.5B (+66% y/y) for OpM of 7.5% (+20bps q/q), which is 100bps/50bps above
MSe/Street, driven by operating leverage. Non-op losses were slightly higher than
MSe/Street mainly due to higher interest expense, resulting in net profit of ~NT
$23.4B (+52% y/y) for EPS of NT$42.09, 44%/10% above MSe/Street.
Reiterate OW with price target unchanged at NT$2,515 (12x/11x CY27/28e P/E) –
our higher EPS estimates are offset by a lower mid-term growth rate. Risk-reward is

highly attractive at 7.7x CY27e or 6.7x CY28e P/E vs. the three-year range of 6-16x,
with a strong pipeline of AI and non-AI server projects.

6
Price target discussion and valuation methodology
We keep our price target unchanged at NT$2,515: This implies 12x CY27e P/E or 11x CY28
P/E, vs. its thee-year average forward P/E of 10.3x, which we think is fair, in view of
Wiwynn's AI server project wins and share gains for general-purpose servers in the next 1-
2 years.
We continue to use a residual income model to derive our price target. Key assumptions
include a cost of equity of 7.0% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 6% and risk-free rate of

1%), a medium-term growth rate of 4% (lowered from 6%), and a terminal growth rate of
3%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values are also unchanged at NT$3,210 and NT$1,470,
respectively.

STOCK RATINGS

Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

文曄(3036) 
26Q4資料中心恢復成長  
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 250 
前次投資建議 2026.8.6： 
「逢低買進」，目標價：$300

報告日期：2026.11.6 研 究 員：林秀泓

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q4 資料中心產品恢復強勁季增，通訊、資料中心產品受益 AI需求。 
➢ 2026 營收翻倍，2027營收增速減緩。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/5 收盤價： 195.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合
財測 QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，
YoY-0.14pt)，符合財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為產品組合
改善。EPS 7.97元。 
➢ 26Q4 文曄財測以美元匯率 32.2 估計，預估營收
7,300~7,770 億元(QoQ+22.2%~+30.1%)，主要由資料中
心出貨恢復帶動。毛利率 3.2%~3.3%。營業費用
7,154~7,546 億元。財測 EPS 8.07~9.05 元。 
➢ 展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子受提前拉貨
影響，預期季減雙位數百分比；工業與儀器因季節性因
素，預期季減中個位數百分比；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需
求，預期季增高雙位數百分比；資料中心與伺服器預期季
增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約兩倍；消費性產品因提前
拉貨，預期季減中雙位數百分比；PC 預期季減；手機旺
季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙位數百分比。 
➢ 調整預估 2026/2027 年營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，
YoY+105.0%/+12.5%，EPS 28.85 元/33.57 元。 
➢ 目前股價處於 6X 27(F) PER，為過去 PE區間 7~40X的
低位。考量 2026 年 AI 帶動營收大幅成長，2027 年 YoY
增速明顯減緩，投資評等維持「逢低買進」 ，調整目標價
250 元(7X 27(F) PER)，潛在上漲空間 28%。

營 運 分 析

文曄為 IC通路代理商 
文曄成立於 1993/12 成立，2002/08 上市掛牌，為提供全球專業電子零組件
通路服務的廠商，透過合併多家 IC通路商的方式持續擴大公司規模。營運總
部設立於台灣，目前在中國、韓國、新加坡、印度、泰國、馬來西亞、越南
等地區共有五十多個營運據點。代理半導體原廠超過 400 家，服務客戶超過
25,000 家。根據 2025 年 Gartner 最新數據，文曄已經成為全球最大和亞太
區最大的半導體通路商，市佔分別為 15.1%和 17.0%。 
產品組合方面，以代理產品的應用劃分營收，26Q3 汽車電子佔 5%、工業與
儀器佔 9%、通訊佔 13%、資料中心及伺服器佔 56%、消費性及其他佔 5%、
個人電腦與週邊佔 4%、手機佔 9%。

26Q3 財報符合財測，獲利再創單季新高 
26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合財測 5,720~6,120
億元(QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%)。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，YoY-0.14pt)，符合
財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為高毛利的工業、車用、Future 營收季增，產品組
合改善。營業費用QoQ+1.6%，費用率1.22%維持低檔，營業槓桿效益持續。
營業利益 142.3 億元，QoQ+9.5%，YoY+151.5%，優於財測中位數 140 億
元。業外方面，淨財務成本約19.4 億元，高於原先預估，因為資金成本上升。
其他業外收入 5.8 億元主要來自權益法認列日電貿的獲利，以及股利收入。
淨利 103.0 億元，QoQ+6.0%，YoY+169.9%，EPS 7.97元。

- 4 - 
26Q4 營收預估季增，資料中心恢復成長 
26Q4 文曄財測以美元匯率 32.2 估計，預估營收 7,300~7,770 億元
(QoQ+22.2%~+30.1%，YoY+113.4%~+127.1%)，主要由資料中心出貨恢復
帶動 。 毛利率3.2%~3.3%。 營業費用7,154~7,546億元 。 財測EPS 8.07~9.05
元。

26Q4 資料中心恢復高雙位數季增，通訊持續成長 
26Q3 分產品線來看(以新台幣計)，汽車電子 QoQ+18%，優於前次法說預期
季減中低個位數百分比；工業與儀器 QoQ+13%，優於預期季增個位數百分
比；通訊 QoQ+5%，符合預期；資料中心與伺服器 QoQ-5%、YoY+114%，
符合預期；消費性產品 QoQ-7%，符合預期；PC週邊 QoQ持平，優於預期
季減個位數百分比，若不含記憶體則QoQ-11%、YoY-20%；手機QoQ+32%，
符合預期，受惠旺季與提前拉貨。 
展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子：受提前拉貨及庫存調整影響，
預期季減雙位數百分比，YoY+20%以上，全年YoY+30%以上；工業與儀器：
季節性因素，預期季減中個位數百分比，YoY+40%以上，全年成長約40%；
通訊：受益於 AI光通訊需求，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約一倍；
資料中心與伺服器：出貨節奏調整後恢復，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年
成長約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；消費性產品：因提前拉貨，預期季
減中雙位數百分比；PC：預期季減；手機：旺季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙
位數百分比。

半導體市場成長再度上修 
CSP 的 AI 資本支出展望正向，AI 需求強且具結構性。根據 Gartner 數據，
2026 年全球半導體市場由原先預估 YoY+92%再度上修到 YoY+108%，2027
年預估 YoY+39.2%(不含記憶體+33%)。半導體市場 2025~2029 年 CAGR
由 25.9%上修到 35.1%，2029 年市場規模預估達 2.7 兆美元。data center
為成長最強勁的領域，2026/2027 年預估 YoY+171%/+60%，2025~2029 年
CAGR+54.9%。 
文曄 26Q3 資料中心受出貨節奏影響，26Q4 恢復強勁季增，2026 全年成長
約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需求，成長動能
延續。Future 方面，歐美市場持續正向復甦，26Q3 營收 YoY+40%以上，車
用與歐洲工廠自動化已無去庫存壓力，資料中心業務也開始接單出貨。Future
較高毛利率可部分抵銷 AI 產品線低毛利率的影響。調整預估 2026/2027 年
營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，YoY+105.0%/+12.5%，淨利 387 億元/450
億元，YoY+185.9%/+16.3%，EPS 28.85 元/33.57 元。

目前股價處於 6X 27(F) PER，為過去 PE區間 7~40X的低位。考量 2026 年
AI 帶動營收大幅成長，2027 年 YoY 增速明顯減緩，投資評等維持「逢低買
進」 ，調整目標價 250 元(7X 27(F) PER)，潛在上漲空間 28%。

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

Underperform(매도) 
비율 93.2% 6.8% 0.0% 
산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

Document 284: AI監管衝擊與選後美股展望-CTBC20261106

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI監管衝擊與選後美股展望-CTBC20261106.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI
監管衝擊與選後美股展望
2026.11.06
美股策略研究
:

厲柏宏

### Page 2

結論與展望
• AI監管風險：民主黨取得國會兩院控制權，AI監管疑慮升溫。2027年資料中心建設進度相對
穩定，但2028年約30GW規劃容量面臨潛在審批風險，恐影響部分專案投產時程。
• 產業影響預期：德州共和黨仍掌握州參議院多數，長期全面禁建面臨立法及司法制衡。過去
約20GW受地方建設限制的容量中，實際延期僅約1.5GW，顯示政策曝險不等於實際損失。
• 政策影響：民主黨可能透過國會制衡財政擴張，中長期若赤字及發債壓力下降，將有利美債
表現。美元預期短空長多，而美伊衝突降溫預期升高，可能使短期油價持續承壓。
• 醫療與零售：民主黨可能爭取恢復部分Medicaid、SNAP及ACA補貼，有助維持中低收入家
庭購買力，支撐消費與醫療需求；惟川普仍掌握關稅行政權，零售業成本改善空間相對有限。
• 美股後市展望：歷史經驗顯示，期中選舉後隨政治不確定性下降，美股通常呈現回升。過去9
次期中選舉後12個月，科技股有8次跑贏大盤，獲利品質、成長及低波動策略亦具相對優勢，
後續仍須關注AI監管落地及企業獲利表現。

1資料來源：中信投顧整理

### Page 3

AI監管提案增加，2028面臨較高審批風險
• 民主黨在期中選舉取得參眾兩院 ，並提出超級 AI禁令及資料中心暫停建設法案，分別涉及限
制AI模型開發，及暫停新建資料中心直至相關機制建立 ，引發對監管趨嚴與投資放緩的擔憂 。
• SemiAnalysis估計，美國2027預計交付的資料中心約有75%已動工，短期進度穩定；惟2028，
規劃多數尚未施工，若暫停建設政策擴大，約30GW新增容量可能面臨審批延後風險。
2資料來源：dcmap.us、中信投顧整理
AI監管風險
美國各州資料中心容量分布與建設進度 (GW)

### Page 4

州級監管受立法制衡，政策曝險不等於實際損失
• 州長行政權仍受州議會及司法制衡 ，以德州為例，共和黨仍掌握州參議院過半席次，全面禁
建制度化仍需跨黨派支持；惟州政府可延續部分許可限制，短期專案進度仍可能受影響。
• 過去一年約20GW受地方建設限制的容量中，實際延誤僅約 1.5GW；預期監管趨嚴將提高開
發成本並延後部分2028專案，短期影響AI硬體訂單與交付，但長期算力需求未必減少。
3資料來源：MultiState Elctions、SemiAnalysis、中信投顧整理
產業影響預期
11/05德州期中選舉統計 地方禁令實際影響有限

### Page 5

國會制衡與美伊局勢緩和，牽動美債及美元走勢
• 民主黨掌控國會後，較可能透過立法及預算權制衡共和黨政策 ，降低進一步財政擴張空間。
短期政策不確定性可能加大債市波動 ，但中長期若赤字與發債壓力下降，將有利美債表現。
而AI投資貢獻美國經濟成長比例加大，故美元表現將與AI股趨勢相同，預期美元短空長多。
• 伊朗戰爭推升汽油價格成為共和黨選舉壓力來源 ，隨民主黨掌控國會提高延續軍事行動的政
治成本，加上美伊持續透過中東國家間接接觸，市場對衝突降溫預期升高，油價短期回落。
4資料來源：中信投顧整理
政策影響
美國總統與國會權力

### Page 6

國會控制權轉向，醫療與消費政策迎來調整空間
• 民主黨可能透過債務上限協商，爭取延後或縮減 Medicaid、SNAP減支，並恢復ACA增額保
費補貼，有助維持中低收入家庭購買力與醫療保險覆蓋率，對民生消費、醫療服務偏正面。
• 關稅也是本次輪替重點，但川普仍握有貿易政策行政權與301條款等工具，整體有效關稅稅率
未必明顯下降；因此零售業獲利改善仍取決於企業定價能力及供應鏈調整成效。
5資料來源：Commonwealth Fund、Uni. of Penn、中信投顧整理
醫療與零售
現行法規預期刪減福利 美國現行關稅預估

### Page 7

期中選舉後政治不確定性下降，歷史上美股通常回升
• 觀察過去數次美國期中選舉，美股通常在選前因政治不確定性升高而面臨較大波動 ，但選後
隨著政策方向逐漸明朗，期中選舉後標普多數呈現上漲趨勢。
• 產業方面，科技股在過去9次期中選舉後的12個月，科技股有8次跑贏標普。平均報酬最高的
選股策略則為獲利品質、成長與低波動類股。
6資料來源：JPM、Barclays、中信投顧整理
歷史統計
標普在期中選舉年表現 各投資策略及產業表現

亦有可能以本報告所涵蓋之標的公司作為發行衍生性金融商品之標的。中信金集團轄下的銷售人

然該等看法與策略可能與本報告的意見不盡一致。中信金集團之自營、資產管理及其他投資業務

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

Chart of the Week / 14 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
tarik.rharrab@bnpparibas.com
With the help of Ece Usta (intern).

Document 357: G10 FX Outlook 2027_ It’s hard to look beyond the Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/G10 FX Outlook 2027_ It’s hard to look beyond the Fed.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

In a very uncertain world, this year’s more hawkish steer from
the Federal Reserve has provided a constant and supportive
anchor for the dollar. While the immediate picture for the dollar
looks quite constructive, a turn in the inﬂation cycle by the
second half of 2027 should usher in a modest downtrend

Chris Turner and Francesco Pesole
Executive summary
We all know that the ‘big dollar’ call sets the tone for global
markets – and that call is largely inﬂuenced by the Fed. What
seems clear as we go to press is that the central bank is not about
to turn dovish. In fact, inﬂuential centrist Christopher Waller has just
made his case for opening the door to a 50bp hike this December.
That hawkish, dollar-positive tilt stands in contrast to the typically
negative seasonal factors faced by the dollar in December. This
year, however, the dollar has enough positives to defy that pattern.
US growth might slow slightly from the 3.4% quarter-on-quarter
annualised rate in the third quarter but remains strong. And what

### Page 2

seems to be a strengthening jobs market and inﬂation way above
target look set to keep tightening expectations ﬁrm for a couple of
months yet. That probably means the low-yielding yen and Swiss
franc can remain on the back foot over the coming months.
It seems a diﬃcult call to make today, but the ING house view
forecasts just one 25bp Fed hike in December to conclude this
recalibration of Fed policy. We are not forecasting an AI bust but are
positioning ourselves at the dovish end of the Fed spectrum for

2027. Here, further pressure on the housing market, alongside
lower energy prices and softer wages should give the Fed enough
room to ease restrictive policy in the ﬁnal months of 2027. Such an
outcome feels a distant prospect right now.
An ‘up then down’ dollar baseline view points to relatively conﬁned
ranges in 2027. But in keeping with the theme of this outlook, ‘Fat
tails, slim ranges’, extreme scenarios need to be considered. We
look at those risks in more detail in other articles in this publication.

Beyond the reaction functions of the Fed and the European Central
Bank, the two most important drivers of EUR/USD will likely be the
path of the AI story and the geopolitical/energy scenario.
We look at the range of possible EUR/USD outcomes in light of those
risks below. As we learned last year, pushing a baseline view too
heavily without suﬃcient regard to foreign policy possibilities can
prove dangerous.

Currency sections
EUR/USD: Hope for the best, prepare for the
worst
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4Q27
EUR/USD 1.11 Bullish 1.13 1.13 1.16 1.17 1.18
Fed stays hawkish through the ﬁrst quarter of 2027: Underpinning
dollar strength through the second half of this year has been the
hawkish Federal Reserve. New Chair Kevin Warsh’s commitment to

restore monetary credibility has put paid to dollar debasement fears.
We look for hawkish Fed pricing to remain the dominant dollar theme
through the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, driven by a solid US labour market,
inﬂation still in the 3.5-4.0% area and AI investment still contributing to
growth. Our baseline sees the dollar turning lower in the second
quarter, when inﬂation will be falling back to 2.0% and money markets
will be questioning whether the Fed needs to keep policy at 100bp

above neutral with inﬂation back at target. This assumes one Fed hike
this December to 4.25% and that the Fed is able to cut rates at the end
of 2027.
ECB to deliver ﬁnal hike in December: The eurozone economy has put
in a resilient performance this year despite higher energy prices. The
European Central Bank (ECB) has already hiked rates twice this year and
is expected to hike to 2.75% in December. That should be enough to
contain inﬂation, which is still largely driven by energy. Second-round

eﬀects are nowhere to be seen. Our team sees eurozone growth slowly

### Page 4

picking up through 2027 to deliver full-year growth of 1.2%. Like the
Fed, we think the ECB will have room to ease restrictive monetary
policy towards the end of 2027. There will also be a focus on who
succeeds ECB President Christine Lagarde by next February. Klaas Knot
and Pablo Hernández de Cos have made it to the ﬁnal round. A sea
change in ECB policy looks unlikely, given that a new chief economist
will balance whoever gets the top job.
EUR/USD baseline and risks: Speculators are well on their way to

building extensive short euro positions. The data suggests the European
buy-side, just as was the case in early 2025, is prepared to hold
positions in US assets with very low FX hedge ratios. Our base case
assumes continued trading in a 1.10-1.15 range over the next four to
ﬁve months and then a modest rebound towards 1.18 by end-2027 as
US inﬂation drops and the Fed loosens restrictive policy. At a time of a
resilient US economy, a lower EUR/USD is probably the more vulnerable

side over the coming months. This could potentially be driven by a
large spike in energy prices or the French budgetary situation curtailing
any further ECB hikes and, in an extreme case, triggering ECB bond
market intervention. The most pressing upside EUR/USD risk is probably
a much deeper stock market correction, with US consumers hit by
wealth eﬀects and the market swinging towards Fed cuts, not hikes.
USD/JPY: Friends in high places
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q

USD/JPY 160.00 Bearish 158.00 158.00 155.00 15
160 remains the line in the sand: Ever since Washington joined Tokyo’s
defence of the yen in late July, USD/JPY has not been able to hold a
move above 160. The ﬁrst joint intervention to support the yen since
1998 sent a strong signal of aligned interests. Japan does not want a
weaker yen to make the cost of living any worse. Washington does not
want a higher USD/JPY to drag the USD/Asia complex with it,

undermining eﬀorts to level the playing ﬁeld for US manufacturers.
Even though the US Treasury only intervened in small amounts of
EUR/JPY at the time, the commitment to keep USD/JPY under 160 looks
clear. A hawkish Fed makes that task harder, but the Bank of Japan (BoJ)
should be closing the rate gap. We look for a hike to 1.50% in December
and then another in March. A pronounced turn lower in USD/JPY may
not emerge until the second quarter of next year, however, when we

have short-dated US rates ﬁnally starting to drop.

### Page 5

Takaichi tries to reﬂate the economy: Prime Minister Sanae Takaichi’s
policies are focused on building strength both domestically and
internationally. Domestically, her party, the Liberal Democratic Party
(LDP), is pursuing a strategic JPY370tr investment target by 2040,
utilising both public and private money, targeted across 17 strategic
sectors. Growing at around 0.8%, the Japanese economy is already
around trend output, and Japan may be one of the few G10 economies

with a positive output gap in 2027. This means the BoJ could be one of
the central banks to hike more than expected or be among the last to
cut rates in a global downtrend. This backdrop is keeping the BoJ
hawkish and the focus on whether corporate proﬁts are still driving
wages higher. Recent evidence suggests they are, which is one of the
reasons why the BoJ has recently concluded that its 2% inﬂation target
has been stably met. A key focus in 2027 will be whether Japan can use

productive investment to oﬀset its labour shortage challenges.
Yen remains cheap: Driven by years of low inﬂation and nominal
depreciation, the real trade-weighted yen remains exceptionally weak.
We estimate that USD/JPY is around 20% overvalued on medium-term
fundamentals, with further yen downside now protected by the Tokyo-
Washington alliance. Our call for 152 next year is largely based on the
dollar decline from spring next year. The surprise package, however,

could be Japan’s domestic story, were the economy to start beneﬁting
more broadly from the AI buildout. Here, Japan’s role is supplying the
hardware, manufacturing technology and infrastructure. We have seen
in Korea this year how the AI export story has broadened into stronger
domestic demand and a more hawkish central bank. Higher growth
rates domestically could be an important piece of the jigsaw that
ﬁnally brings home a more substantial part of Japanese earnings

retained overseas.
GBP/USD: Another year of tight ranges
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
GBP/USD 1.33 Mildly Bullish 1.36 1.36 1.38 1.38 1
BoE matches Fed hawkishness: One abiding feature of core money
markets this year was how readily traders were prepared to price in
Bank of England (BoE) hikes. Sterling rates did have one of the highest
correlations with oil prices, but even when Brent corrected below
$100/bbl recently, markets still held onto views of a terminal BoE rate

of near 4.75%. Important for the GBP/USD story is the timing of the
relative US and UK inﬂation trajectories in 2027. Our team sees US

### Page 6

inﬂation coming back towards 2% in the second quarter as energy base
eﬀects drop out. In the UK, however, the lagged implementation of the
energy price caps means that inﬂation does not start to turn lower until
the third quarter. Our call is therefore that US rates could turn lower
before those in the UK, and that GBP/USD could be retesting the 1.37-
1.38 range highs in the second quarter.
Some creeping optimism: It might not seem like it given that GBP/USD

is still trapped in the middle of its current 1.32-1.37 range, but investors
are getting more positive on the pound. The price of the one-year 25-
delta risk reversal, which measures the price of a call option over a put
option, is now showing the smallest skew towards GBP puts since March.
That has not shown up in the FX positioning data yet, where asset
managers are still running large sterling shorts by historical standards.
Buy-side surveys also show international fund managers are still

substantially underweight in UK equities. But positioning does present
an upside risk to GBP/USD as we head into year-end. If it were to break
higher, we suspect it might be driven more by surprise weakness in the
dollar, however. This is because the BoE tightening cycle into next year
looks fully priced.
Fiscal beneﬁts: In a world where ﬁscal trajectories are being
increasingly scrutinised, the UK falls on the more encouraging side of
the ledger. At least the direction of travel looks encouraging for the

budget deﬁcit, while the fact that the UK has a ﬁscal rule, unlike the US,
is welcome. We also note that the UK has quite a healthy duration on
its government debt stock at 13.5 years, compared with 5.9 years for
the US. The UK government’s reﬁnancing requirements as a percentage
of GDP are much smaller at 8.7%, according to the IMF, compared with
30.8% for the US. Whether sterling would outperform the dollar in a
major bond market sell-oﬀ remains to be seen, but the UK’s debt

maturity proﬁle and near-term debt ﬁnancing needs certainly look
better than those in the US.
EUR/JPY: Steady, with downside risks
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q
EUR/JPY 177.00 Neutral 179.00 179.00 180.00 17
Positive correlation with growth and equities: Both Europe and Japan
have faced their struggles with high energy prices, but both economies
have performed quite well. Europe has beneﬁted from the growing
ﬁscal stimulus put in place last year, which is starting to show through

### Page 7

in series like German manufacturing orders. Japan’s economy has been
protected by the government’s use of energy stockpiles, and in 2027,
Japanese consumers will beneﬁt from a large cut in VAT on food next
April. Yet EUR/JPY remains positively correlated with growth and pro-risk
sentiment in general and would suﬀer in an equity downturn. Decent
advances in corporate proﬁts suggest that a large equity correction is
not a given, while forecasts for continued growth and a turn in inﬂation

in the second half of 2027 make the case for continued equity gains
and a supported EUR/JPY.
Focus on France: Japanese investors have historically been big
investors in French sovereign bond markets, although these ﬂows seem
to have stalled over recent years. French debt had been seen as a
higher-yielding, liquid alternative to German Bunds. However, France’s
unresolved budgetary situation and the threat of further sovereign
downgrades urge caution. Next up is the S&P rating review on 27

November. Our base case is that the current French government passes
a 2027 budget by decree and that some modest ﬁscal consolidation is
enough to avoid dramatic downgrades. This is no 2011-2012 situation,
when the market was shut to Greek sovereign issuance. Were we to be
underestimating the pressure on French debt, the solution would be
euro-negative, especially if the ECB needed to use its Transmission
Protection Instrument, which would eﬀectively mean restarting

quantitative easing. Additionally, some slight narrowing in eurozone-
Japan interest rates might encourage Japanese investors to increase
hedge ratios on euro-denominated debt holdings, which would be a
EUR/JPY negative.
Yen or euro the preferred funding currency? Inevitably, there will be
periods in 2027 when cross-market volatility declines and investors
again return to the carry trade. But what will the preferred funding
currency be in the G10 space? The Swiss franc has the lowest borrowing

rates, but Japanese rates should be closing in on euro rates by the end
of 2027. At that point, we could be looking at the BoJ policy rate near
1.75-2.00% and the ECB rate at 2.50%. Unless eurozone growth sharply
surprises on the upside and there is a substantial rotation into euro-
denominated asset markets, we suspect investors will be considering
euro-funded carry trades given the nature of European political risk in
2027. Assuming inﬂation does turn lower through 2027, we expect the

ECB to be more welcoming of a weaker euro than Japan is of a weaker
yen. Overall, we see a relatively ﬂat EUR/JPY through 2027 but, again,
with greater risk to the downside.
EUR/GBP: Let’s not get carried away

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
EUR/GBP 0.84 Mildly Bullish 0.83 0.83 0.84 0.85
Sterling has overshot rate diﬀerentials: EUR/GBP is already trading
substantially lower than interest rate diﬀerentials would suggest. Some
of that is probably down to a risk premium in the euro on the back of
French budgetary risk. Sterling may also be playing a limited role as a
safe haven at the moment, given it trades at lower volatility than many
G10 FX pairs, oﬀers high interest rates and has a powerful ﬁscal rule,

which means that in 2027 the UK budget deﬁcit should narrow to close
to 3% of GDP. At the same time, we think ongoing merger and
acquisition inﬂows to the UK are playing a role here too. £141bn of deal
ﬂows have already been completed year-to-date, and there’s another
£260bn of pending or proposed inﬂows. That said, historic correlations
with yield spreads suggest EUR/GBP should be trading closer to 0.86,
meaning that current levels are stretched.

Low volatility to continue: The current analyst consensus for EUR/GBP
is 0.86 for end-2027, with a range of forecasts stretching from 0.82 to
0.91. One of the biggest trends in this pair over recent years has been
the structural decline in volatility following the political turbulence
surrounding Brexit. One-year implied volatility is trading below 5%, with
realised volatility trading well below 4%. That low volatility and the
relatively high cost of hedging are presumably keeping hedge ratios low

on sterling receivables. Assuming that the eurozone and UK economies
move broadly in line next year, we see both growing at around 1.2%
and doubt volatility needs to pick up markedly. The key threat to this
benign environment is probably political, where risks could emerge
from both sides of the Channel.
Unwinding Brexit? One wild card for the pound is the risk that Prime
Minister Andy Burnham opts for early elections next year. Labour is
performing well in the polls, and long-term government objectives on

social care and getting closer to Europe look like they will require a
mandate from the British electorate. How close the Labour Party wants
to get to Europe remains to be seen. At present, Labour policy wants
merely to start a conversation on the subject. But were Labour to put
another referendum in its election manifesto, sterling would take a lot
more positive notice. That said, an early election might not be a one-
sided risk for sterling if, say, the ﬁscal rule were to come up for

renegotiation.
EUR/CHF: SNB more tolerant of franc strength

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/CHF 0.92 Mildly Bearish 0.91 0.91 0.90 0.90
Franc plays important role in SNB policy: Having proved a popular
funding currency during the summer, the rebound in the franc and the
drop in EUR/CHF have been driven by lower core rates and the re-
emergence of French budgetary risk. So far, the Swiss National Bank
(SNB) has sounded happy to keep the policy rate at zero even while the
rest of the world is tightening. Our house call remains for the SNB to

keep rates unchanged, and we doubt it would tighten even if inﬂation
nudged above 2% for a couple of months. However, much lower
inﬂation in Switzerland than in its trading partners means that the real
franc has been weakening. The SNB does not want the extra monetary
stimulus of a weaker real exchange rate right now, and we suspect it
will be happy with some nominal franc appreciation. We are not calling
for a repeat of the FX selling by the SNB in 2022-2023 but saying that

another run at 0.90 in EUR/CHF may actually suit the central bank.
Less concern over a strong franc: The minutes from the most recent
SNB meeting in September reported that companies were mentioning
the Swiss franc a lot less in their conversations with central bank
agents. In the past, the SNB had come under a lot of pressure from the
corporate sector over a strong franc, but these concerns seem to have
eased. This may be a function of the strong Swiss economy this year,

where growth is expected to come in at 1.5-2.0% this year and 1.5%
next year. The pharma sector has been a big contributor here and is
seen exporting around $115bn in 2026. Our sector experts expect a
compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 4.5% until 2030, helped in part
by biologics. Also helping the sector are the exemptions from the 12.5-
15% US tariﬀs on the sector struck by major Swiss pharma companies
in return for investment in US manufacturing.

A preferred safe haven: The franc strengthened its credentials as a safe
haven during both 2025 and 2026. A solid AAA-rated issuer, twin
surpluses and a net foreign asset position worth over 100% of GDP
make the franc a go-to choice in the event of either a bond sell-oﬀ or
European political risk, both of which are likely to be present in 2027. At
the same time, the SNB has backed away from its "increased
willingness to intervene", a phrase introduced when EUR/CHF brieﬂy

broke below 0.90 in March. In reality, that threat had overstated the
SNB’s intentions, given that it only bought CHF4bn in the ﬁrst quarter
and under CHF2bn in the second. We therefore expect EUR/CHF to stay

oﬀered through much of 2027, with a risk of a break below 0.90 in the
event of a crisis.
EUR/SEK: Gliding back to pre-war levels
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q
EUR/SEK 11.05 Mildly Bearish 11.00 10.90 10.90 10
Riksbank’s tightening cycle should be limited: The Riksbank has clearly
communicated that its baseline is for rates to reach 2.25-2.50% from
the current 2.0% by mid-2027. We agree, and while the risks remain
ﬁnely balanced between one and two more hikes, we are sticking to our

call for just one in February or March after the latest inﬂation numbers
materially undershot expectations. Still, our forecasts point to some
upside risk to the Riksbank’s 2027 inﬂation projections, with CPIF
excluding energy and temporary ﬁscal measures - the central bank’s
favoured measure - potentially peaking at 2.5-2.7% in 2Q, compared
with its 2.3% baseline. Combined with a still-solid Swedish growth
outlook, that makes our Riksbank call one of the most exposed to

hawkish revisions in the coming months. Importantly, we don’t expect
conditions for a rate cut to emerge before 2028 and estimate the long-
run neutral rate at 2.25%.
Medium- and short-term factors point to a moderate EUR/SEK
decline: Rate diﬀerentials have been the name of the game recently for
EUR/SEK. In line with this FX Outlook’s call, we think this will remain the
case next year and prove a net negative for the pair. The two-year
overnight index swap (OIS) rate gap is currently around 40bp, down

from a peak of around 60bp in September. We expect the gap to narrow
by 15-30bp over the course of 2027. The range reﬂects whether the
Riksbank hikes once or twice, as well as markets switching more quickly
to pricing in ECB rate cuts. We expect one last ECB hike in December
2026 and a cut in the fourth quarter of 2027. The growth diﬀerential
should also remain moderately negative for EUR/SEK. We estimate
2027 growth at 2.1% in Sweden versus 1.2% in the eurozone. That

should favour a gradual return to pre-war ranges in the pair.
Risks to our baseline: We see EUR/SEK stabilising around 11.00 into
end-2026, before gradually returning to 10.80 in 2027. Risks of a bigger
decline stem more from the euro side, as a busy eurozone political
calendar could drive further widening in European government bond
spreads and, in turn, looser ECB policy. Upside risks are mostly
geopolitical. Our baseline assumes Brent returns to $70-80/bbl in 2027,

a clearly optimistic scenario. SEK is less exposed to higher oil prices
now, given the Riksbank’s readiness to tighten, but a prolonged Gulf
conﬂict would still argue for a more supported EUR/SEK. The inﬂation
ramiﬁcations are particularly important: a return to a 2022-style global
inﬂationary crisis can cause SEK losses to exceed those of 2026. A
bigger threat to the krona is a potential escalation between Russia and
NATO, given Sweden’s geographical exposure. An AI bust also poses

upside risks for EUR/SEK if contagion spreads across European markets,
though SEK should be better protected in a US-idiosyncratic event
thanks to capital repatriation ﬂows.
EUR/NOK: A year for stabilisation
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/NOK 10.59 Neutral 10.65 10.70 10.70 10.80
Cuts to start in 2H27: We think the bar for another Norges Bank hike is
fairly high. While Norway had an inﬂation problem before the Iran war,
policy was already restrictive at the start of 2026. Policymakers aren’t

ruling out another move but have sounded content with the current
4.5% rate and are mindful of overtightening risks. We broadly agree
with Norges Bank’s latest projections, which see headline and
underlying (ATE) inﬂation falling steadily below 3.0% from 1Q27 and
stabilising around 2.3-2.8% in the second half of next year. Restrictive
policy even before the energy shock, alongside krone appreciation,
limits the risk of further inﬂation overshooting. Policymakers will be

wary of jumping the gun on easing, but a roughly 2% positive real rate
will argue for some unwinding of tightening in 2H27. We forecast two
25bp cuts, in 3Q and 4Q, taking rates to 4.0%. Some growth softening
should contribute to the dovish shift: we expect 1.2% growth in 2027,
with downside risks.
A quieter year for NOK: Markets aren’t fully pricing in rate cuts in
Norway before 2028, so we expect a dovish repricing to weigh on the
krone. At the same time, we doubt Norges Bank will signal a rapid

cutting cycle ahead, as inﬂation will still be above the 2% target in
2027. Incidentally, our view leans on the dovish side of consensus, and
the risks are skewed towards a delay in easing plans. In the ﬁrst half of
the year, anyway, NOK will continue to enjoy a very attractive carry. If
our call for low volatility materialises, carry should limit the size of any
EUR/NOK rally fuelled by lower energy prices. As we see the ECB shift to
easing from 4Q27, the front-end rate diﬀerential in EUR/NOK should turn

only moderately more supportive for the pair. Expectations for lower

### Page 12

interest rates in the US too should also make for a broadly supportive
environment for the low-liquidity NOK. Finally, NOK’s stronger
fundamentals relative to the euro argue against large EUR/NOK
appreciation.
Geopolitics and equities the main risks: While our baseline is for a
quiet year for EUR/NOK, the krone is the least liquid currency in G10,
leaving it highly exposed to violent corrections in equities, like those
seen in autumn 2026. Geopolitical developments will remain a key

source of volatility risk for EUR/NOK. Any re-escalation and prolonged
conﬂict in the Middle East should bring initial beneﬁts to the krone if
energy prices ﬁnd a new, higher ﬂoor. But if inﬂation causes another
round of monetary tightening across the globe, expect NOK to
underperform, as liquidity conditions drying up and more elevated
volatility would nullify the krone’s rate advantage. The biggest black
swan for NOK downside remains any deterioration in Russia-NATO

relations.
EUR/DKK: Moderately stronger for longer
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4Q27
EUR/DKK 7.48 Neutral 7.47 7.47 7.47 7.47 7.46
Greater tolerance for EUR/DKK upside: Danmarks Nationalbank (DN)
has signalled greater tolerance for EUR/DKK appreciation this year.
Between October 2019 and January 2020, before the Covid impact,
levels around 7.470-7.475 prompted DKK17.8bn of FX sales to defend
the krone. Similar levels have prevailed throughout this year, with peaks

above 7.476, yet intervention has been much smaller at DKK6.3bn,
spread between June and August. The deviations from the 7.460 peg
target remain very small, reaching around 0.22% at the intraday peak
in July. Still, the signal points to a partial change in the central bank’s
tolerance for EUR/DKK upside drifts.
Intervention to remain ﬁrst line of defence: For some time, we sensed
that contained FX intervention might have been a prelude to larger DN

rate increases versus the ECB, providing structural support to the krone.
Instead, the bar for a rate reaction by DN seems to have been set
higher. Incidentally, even the small FX sales seen this year have been
enough to take the edge oﬀ EUR/DKK, indicating good eﬀectiveness.
Denmark's FX reserves have plateaued in USD terms in 2026, but at
around $110bn (approximately 22% of GDP), they still provide very
large ammunition to protect the DKK peg. We therefore expect FX

intervention to remain the ﬁrst line of defence against EUR/DKK upward
volatility. That could be associated with renewed US-Denmark
diplomatic clashes over Greenland, although the midterm blow to
President Trump has probably reduced that risk.
Upward drift can linger for longer: In our view, the +40bp policy rate
diﬀerential between the ECB and DN should prevent EUR/DKK from
trading back below central peg parity (7.460) next year. Unless DN
closes that gap through surprise rate moves, we expect EUR/DKK to

remain broadly supported in a 7.465-7.475 range for most of 2027.
Ultimately, we feel that a narrower policy rate gap is a realistic longer-
term expectation (2028 is our best guess), which should anchor
EUR/DKK back to 7.460 more steadily.
USD/CAD: A decent outlook for the loonie
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4Q27
USD/CAD 1.42 Bearish 1.41 1.39 1.37 1.35 1.34
BoC to remain cautious: We expect only one more Bank of Canada
(BoC) hike, likely in 1Q27, taking the policy rate to 2.75%. We admit

there are risks of an additional increase to 3.0%, but for now we view
rates at that level as unlikely. Core inﬂation has remained anchored at
2.0% since the start of the Iran war, despite US tariﬀs. The risk of
second-round eﬀects has increased, as lagged supply chain inﬂation
can start to feed through in early 2027, but some factors should keep
these eﬀects relatively contained in our view. Unemployment has
edged lower in 2026, but this reﬂects a further decline in the

participation rate rather than employment strength, with net
employment modestly contracting. Businesses continue to report
muted hiring conditions and no major pickup in expected wage growth,
which has incidentally fallen in 2026 according to oﬃcial data. Trade-
related uncertainty is also weighing on sentiment, while growth
momentum should slow in the new year, limiting businesses' ability to
pass through higher producer prices.
Front-end to favour USD/CAD softening: We expect the Fed to start

cutting rates in late 2027, dragging front-end USD rates markedly lower.
CAD short-term rates are the most correlated with the US in G10, which
often means the loonie lags most other peers during a dovish Fed
repricing. Still, BoC expectations are not aggressive, with markets
currently embedding another 50bp of tightening over the next year,
similar to the USD curve. Crucially, we estimate Fed rates are currently

around 75bp above neutral, broadly in line with FOMC dot plot
projections. In contrast, the midpoint of BoC staﬀ neutral rate estimates
is 2.75%, arguing against any monetary policy easing even if the Fed
starts cutting in late 2027. Improved growth in 2027, also thanks to a
pick-up in AI-related investment, should also play a role. We expect the
USD/CAD 2yr swap rate gap to retighten to pre-war levels, warranting a
gradual decline to 1.34 by end-2027.

Trump remains the big risk for CAD: Markets have largely dismissed the
escalation in the US-Canada trade spat in 2026, with the negative
impact on CAD remaining very contained. The US midterm result may
suggest that the widely unpopular trade war with Canada will be
shelved, increasing the chance of a comprehensive deal ahead of the
yearly United States-Mexico-Canada Agreement (USMCA) review due in
July 2027. Our baseline is for a new USMCA deal to be agreed this year,

although negotiations may be complicated and potentially involve
threats of new US tariﬀs on Canada. This may make for a bumpy path
towards an eventual CAD-positive outcome. Elsewhere, CAD may
emerge as a commodity currency of choice should geopolitical or trade
risks ﬂare up in Europe or Asia. Conversely, the risk of an AI bust could
hit CAD more than its European peers in our view, given the Canadian
economy's exposure to US activity.
AUD/USD: RBA cuts to limit upside

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
AUD/USD 0.68 Mildly Bullish 0.69 0.69 0.70 0.70
RBA to shift to cuts in 2H27: We think the Reserve Bank of Australia
(RBA) is done with hikes after having taken the policy rate to 4.85%, the
highest in the G10. While policymakers haven’t entirely shut the door to
further tightening, the bar looks high as growth and housing concerns
strengthen the dovish case. Restrictive ﬁnancial conditions, housing
weakness and softer conﬁdence should weigh on domestic demand,

while data centre investment is unlikely to provide a suﬃcient oﬀset.
We expect unemployment to reach 4.8-5.0% in 2027 and rise further in
2028. We think that will lead to a shift to easing earlier than most other
central banks, with 25bp cuts in both 3Q27 and 4Q27. That would be the
start of a substantial easing cycle in our view, leading to a terminal rate
of 3.60% by end-2028. While the Australian dollar is likely to start the
year with some of the best risk-adjusted carry in the G10, its rate

attractiveness should deteriorate throughout the year.

### Page 15

Fundamentals favour stable outlook: We forecast the rate impact to be
a modest net-positive for AUD/USD, though. Markets are not pricing in
any extra tightening by the RBA, while already factoring in a cut by
end-2027. Instead, the USD curve is far more mispriced on the hawkish
side, in our view. We expect a roughly +20bp move in the AUD/USD
short-term rate diﬀerential over the next 12 months, which is
consistent with a sustainable return to 0.71 by the end of the year.

However, we expect AUD to lag many G10 peers, especially in the
second half of the year when RBA cuts will dent its carry attractiveness.
We also see Australia’s terms of trade moderating lower, in line with our
view for lower oil, natural gas and iron ore prices. On the latter, our
commodities team forecasts an average of $90-95/t on the back of new
supply from West Africa hitting the market.
AI the biggest risk for AUD: The Aussie dollar’s main vulnerability is

undoubtedly the external risk environment, especially the threat of a
tech-led equity selloﬀ. Recent episodes have consistently seen AUD
underperform most other G10 currencies, and we expect its high
implied yield to keep the Aussie a high-beta currency throughout next
year. We therefore see less scope for AUD to beneﬁt from idiosyncratic
US asset underperformance, particularly compared with low yielders.
The Aussie also remains exposed to any deterioration in China-related

sentiment. While our baseline view for China is growth stabilisation, any
renewed Sino-America trade tensions or geopolitical risk emerging in
Asia could trigger a material proxy-response by AUD.
NZD/USD: Ample room for recovery
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4Q2
NZD/USD 0.56 Bullish 0.56 0.57 0.58 0.59 0.60
Two more hikes by RBNZ: The Reserve Bank of New Zealand (RBNZ)
surprised with a split-vote hold in October, when markets and
consensus were fully aligned for a hike. Half of the committee holds a

rather pessimistic view on domestic and international growth prospects
and will be wary of overtightening. That view was partly validated by a
soft 3Q jobs market report. That said, the inﬂation picture continues to
argue for higher rates, in our view. We see upside risks to the RBNZ’s
latest inﬂation projections that show headline inﬂation gliding back to
2.1% by end-2027. The Bank’s projections already embed one hike from
the current 2.75% and split chances of another afterwards. Our call is

for a hike in December and another in March, reaching a 3.25%
terminal rate. The risks are skewed to a later resumption of hikes, but

we think a material downward surprise in growth would be required to
prevent at least two hikes. Importantly, we don’t currently expect any

Rate support should improve for NZD: We expect a material decline in
USD front-end rates. The Kiwi dollar’s swap curve is also more hawkish
than our expectations, embedding 3.50% for one year ahead and even
another hike two years ahead. At some point in 2027, we think market
expectations for the RBNZ will be revised lower and will start to price in
a long-term neutral rate around 3.0-3.25%. Still, there should be a
material improvement in the short-term rate diﬀerential for NZD/USD,

which should drive spot back to 0.60 next year, in our estimates. The
starting point is incidentally one of short-term undervaluation, with the
pair’s recent selloﬀ larger than warranted by market fundamentals.
NZD would remain a low-yielding alternative to AUD, therefore limiting
idiosyncratic strength, but the gap should start closing as we see
markets pricing in a substantial RBA rate-cutting cycle from 2H27, while
the RBNZ’s lower starting point should curb dovish speculation. We

expect a return to the 1.15-1.20 range in AUD/NZD.
Double exposure to geopolitics and AI: The Kiwi dollar will remain
vulnerable to escalations in the Middle East due to its net-negative
exposure to higher energy prices. Any deterioration in China-related
sentiment remains another risk, although we’d expect a slightly smaller
impact on NZD than AUD, given Australia’s higher dependency on
Chinese demand. We have a similar assessment with regard to an AI-

bust scenario. NZD would be among the worst performers given its high
beta to risk sentiment, but AUD may suﬀer bigger losses as the RBA has
further room to cut in an adverse environment. We think the upside
risks to our NZD baseline forecast mostly stem from the domestic
activity/inﬂation story in New Zealand proving much stronger than
expected, leaving greater room for the RBNZ to tackle elevated
inﬂation with interest rate increases.

Document 393: OPECプラスは増産見送りを継続、2027年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 ～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～

Source PDF: all report pdf/OPECプラスは増産見送りを継続、2027年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 ～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/4 
OPEC プラスは増産見送りを継続、2027 年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 
～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ OPEC プラス有志７ヵ国は11 月 1 日のオンライン会合で、12 月の原油生産目標を据え置き、
３ヵ月連続で増産を見送った。７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万バレルである。 
○ 据え置きの背景には、イラン情勢の緊迫化がある。ホルムズ海峡などで原油輸送に支障が生
じており、目標を引き上げても実際の供給増にはつながりにくい。ＩＥＡによれば、９月の
湾岸産油国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、前月から増えたものの緊迫化前の約４分
の３にとどまる。 石油製品やＬＰＧの輸出は戦前の半分以下にとどまるほか、10 月には中東
のエネルギー関連施設や船舶への攻撃も再び強まった。 
○ 供給制約により原油価格は高水準で推移し、運賃や保険料の上昇も調達コストを押し上げて
いる。 一方、 高値の長期化は世界経済を下押しし、 需要減退を招く懸念がある。ＩＥＡは 2026
年の世界石油需要を前年比で日量260 万バレル減と見込んでいる。 
○ 今後の焦点は、11 月 29 日に予定される OPEC プラス閣僚会合での 2027 年の生産枠の協議と
なる。日量約 200 万バレルの協調減産の扱いや、新たな生産基準の設定が論点だが、イラン
情勢が評価作業を複雑にしている。ＩＥＡは 2027 年に供給が日量770 万バレル増える一方、
需要の回復は同 200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、供給回復後には需給が緩む可能性
がある。ただし、OPEC とＩＥＡの需要見通しには隔たりがあり、先行きは不透明である。 
○ 日本など輸入国にとっても、原油高の長期化は貿易収支の悪化やインフレ圧力の増幅につな
がる。当面は、OPEC プラスの会合内容、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の生産・輸出
動向、世界の石油需要を注視する必要がある。

【OPECプラス有志７ヵ国は12 月の生産目標を据え置き、３ヵ月連続で増産を見送る決定】 
OPEC（石油輸出国機構）にロシアなどの主要産油国を加えた OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国（サウジ
アラビア、ロシア、イラク、クウェート、カザフスタン、アルジェリア、オマーン）は、11 月１日
にオンライン形式で会合を開催し、12 月の原油生産目標を11 月から据え置くことを決定した。こ
れにより、３ヵ月連続で増産が見送られることとなった。 ７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万

### Page 2

2/4 
バレルとなり、９月以来の水準を維持する。今回の声明では、世界の原油市場の動向や先行きを検
討したうえで生産目標の据え置きを決定したと説明するとともに、 各国が協調減産の枠組みを順守
する姿勢をあらためて確認した。

【イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う供給制約を背景に、生産目標と実際の生産量の乖離が続いている】 
OPEC プラス有志国は、2023 年４月に発表し、 翌５月から実施した日量165 万バレルの自主減産に
ついて、 段階的な縮小を進めてきた。2026 年９月には、 この自主減産の縮小を完了したものの、 そ
の後は 10 月、11 月と生産目標を据え置いており、今回も同様の判断を下した。背景には、イラン
情勢の緊迫化を受けて中東地域からの
原油輸送に支障が生じており、 各国が生
産目標を引き上げても、 実際の供給拡大
につながりにくい状況が続いているこ
とがある（図１）。 特に、 世界有数の原油
輸送の要衝であるホルムズ海峡では、 船
舶の航行を巡る安全上の懸念が残り、 原
油輸送に制約が生じている。 湾岸産油国
は代替輸送ルートの活用などによって
輸出の回復を図っているものの、 多くの
国の原油生産量は依然として生産目標
を下回っている。 こうした状況では、 生産目標を引き上げても実際の供給拡大につながる効果は限
定的であり、有志国は現行の生産目標を維持することが妥当と判断したと考えられる。 
実際、ＩＥＡ（国際エネルギー機関）が公表した最新の石油市場月報によれば、９月の湾岸産油
国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、 前月から同440 万バレル増加したものの、 イラン情勢が
緊迫化する以前の水準の４分の３程度にとどまる。さらに、原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品や
ＬＰＧ（液化石油ガス）の輸出量は依然として戦前の半分以下にとどまり、供給制約の解消には至
っていない。10 月には中東地域のエネルギー関連施設や船舶を対象とする攻撃が再び強まり、 輸送
を巡る不確実性も高まっている。 このため、OPEC プラスが生産目標を引き上げても、 それが実際の
原油供給量の増加に結び付くとは限らず、 足元の供給不足を解消するうえでは、 イラン情勢の安定
化と原油輸送の正常化が不可欠といえる。

【原油市場では供給制約が価格を下支えする一方、高値の長期化による需要減退も懸念】 
こうした供給制約は、国際原油市況を
押し上げている（図２）。足元の原油価格
は、イラン情勢が緊迫化する以前の水準
を大きく上回るなど、原油市場では供給
不足への警戒が根強く残っている。さら
に、原油価格の高止まりに加えて海上輸
送に伴う運賃や保険料なども上昇してお
り、原油や石油製品の調達コストを押し
上げる要因となっている。こうした状況
の下では、 仮にOPEC プラスが増産に踏み
切ったとしても、供給制約が残る限り原
油価格の大幅な下落にはつながりにくいと考えられる。 
一方、 原油価格の高止まりが世界経済の下押し圧力となることも懸念される。 原油高に伴うエネ
ルギー価格の上昇は、家計の実質購買力を低下させるとともに、企業の生産・輸送コストを押し上
げることで、個人消費や設備投資などの重しとなる。IEA は、最新の石油市場月報において、2026
年の世界の石油需要が前年比で日量260 万バレル減少するとの見通しを示しており、 供給制約に伴
う石油製品の供給不足や価格高騰などが需要を下押しすると見込んでいる。 このように需要の減退
が進めば、 将来的には原油需給が緩和する可能性もある。 そのため、OPEC プラスにとっては、足元
の供給制約への対応だけでなく、 原油高が世界経済や石油需要に及ぼす影響にも配慮した政策運営
が求められる。

【今後の焦点は2027 年の生産枠設定、供給回復に伴う需給緩和の可能性も課題】 
先行きについては、OPEC プラスが11 月29 日に予定する次回会合での協議が注目される。 今回の
有志７ヵ国による会合とは異なり、次回はOPEC プラス全体の閣僚会合が予定されており、2027 年
の生産枠を巡る協議が主要な焦点になるとみられる。OPEC プラスは、2023 年４月の自主減産につ
いては９月に縮小を完了したものの、 別途実施している日量約200 万バレルの協調減産措置は2026
年末まで維持している。2027 年以降の政策運営を巡っては、 この減産措置の取り扱いが重要な論点
となる。さらに、加盟各国の持続可能な最大生産能力に関する評価を踏まえ、新たな生産基準を設
定する方針であるものの、 イラン情勢の悪化に伴う生産設備や生産能力の拡張計画への影響などが
評価作業を複雑化させている。 
その意味では、今後の OPEC プラスの政策運営は一段と難しさを増すと予想される。イラン情勢
の緊迫化が続き、 原油輸送の制約が長期化すれば、 世界的な供給不足が続くことで原油価格には上
昇圧力が掛かりやすい。一方で、停戦協議の進展などをきっかけに原油輸送が正常化すれば、これ
まで供給が制約されてきた原油が市場に流入することにより、 需給環境が一転して緩和する可能性
がある。ＩＥＡは2027 年の世界の石油供給量が前年比で日量770 万バレル増加すると見込む一方、

需要の回復幅は同200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、 供給回復の進展に伴う需給緩和の可能
性を示唆している。もっとも、OPEC（日量１億 830 万バレル）とＩＥＡ（同１億 440 万バレル）の

ラスにとっては、供給不足が続く現状と、将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらみながら、加盟国間の利
害を調整していく必要がある。
日本をはじめとする原油輸入国にとっても、 原油需給を巡る不確実性は景気や物価の先行きを左
右する重要な要因となる。 原油価格の高止まりは、 輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支に下押し圧力を
もたらすほか、 エネルギー価格や物流コストの上昇を通じてインフレ圧力を増幅させる懸念がある。

一方、 原油輸送の正常化によって供給量が回復すれば、 原油価格の下落を通じてインフレ圧力が和
らぐことも期待される。ただし、当面は中東情勢を巡る不透明感が根強く、原油価格は地政学的な
動向に左右されやすい状況が続くと考えられる。今後は、次回の OPEC プラス閣僚会合での協議内
容に加え、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の実際の生産・輸出動向、世界の石油需要の変化
などを注意深く見極める必要がある。
